# Build plan

The live status of the build. Update this file as steps close — it is the one
place that answers "where are we?" after a context clear.

**Architecture:** [`docs/adr/ADR-035`](adr/ADR-035-target-architecture-postgres-react-native.md).
Where this file and the ADR disagree, the ADR wins and this file is the bug.

**New here, or not a developer?** [`docs/HANDBOOK.md`](HANDBOOK.md) explains the
tooling, the working loop, and where Claude is likely to be wrong.

**Stack:** Postgres (Supabase) + React Native (Expo). Mexico-based small retailers,
MXN, IVA. Spanish module names are the domain vocabulary: Comprar (buy), Vender
(sell), Productos (catalog), Proveedores (providers), Desperdicio (waste),
Números (reports).

**Not the stack:** Power Apps Canvas + Dataverse. That era stopped 2026-08-14 and
lives in [`archive/power-platform/`](../archive/power-platform/README.md), excluded
from the knowledge graph. Nothing there describes the system being built.

---

## Position

### ⛔ DECISIONS OWED BY THE OWNER

⚠️ **THIS BLOCK EXISTS SO NOBODY HAS TO REMEMBER ANYTHING.** Every session reads
`## Position` first, and the working prompt ends by asking what decision is needed — so a
decision parked here is re-offered **every session, automatically**, until it is ruled on.
⚠️ **It is the ONE home for open owner decisions**, and `docs/checks/plan-handover.sh`
refuses a second one, refuses a row that does not say what it blocks, and ⚠️⚠️ **refuses to
let a blocked task be marked as the next task.**

| Decision | Blocks | The brief, already written |
|---|---|---|

⚠️ **Rulings 1–45 and their briefs moved 2026-09-29 (`8b`) to
[`docs/plan/archive/position-history-through-2026-09-29.md`](plan/archive/position-history-through-2026-09-29.md).**
A decision ruled is removed from the table and its record goes to the status log.

### ⏳ DATES OWED — what a calendar owes, and no person is holding

⚠️⚠️ **THIS BLOCK EXISTS BECAUSE THE DECISIONS BLOCK DID NOT COVER DATES, AND ON 2026-09-13
THERE WERE THREE LIVE ONES HELD ONLY BY PROSE.** A decision waits until someone rules; **a
date passes whether or not anyone looked**, and a missed reading is not recoverable by
trying harder afterwards — it is recoverable only by starting the clock again.

⚠️ **A DATE IS NOT A TASK.** It carries no next-task marker, it is not sized, and it must
never be what a cleared session "takes" — the next task is always in the tables below.

⚠️⚠️ **AND THIS BLOCK HAS TEETH THE DECISIONS BLOCK DOES NOT: `plan-handover.sh` FAILS once
a due date is in the PAST and its row is not ticked.** A red here blocks every merge, which
is deliberate and is the only reason a date survives a context clear — **and the exit is ten
seconds: tick the box and write what was read.** ⚠️ It fires only on dates **strictly
before** today (UTC), so a due-today row is never a false red on a timezone.

| Due | What | Why it cannot simply be moved | Done |
|---|---|---|---|
| **2026-10-04** | ⚠️⚠️ **RE-DEPLOY WERA TO THE IPHONE — THE SUCCESSOR TO THE 2026-09-27 ROW, AND IT NOW CARRIES THE PREREQUISITE THAT ROW DISCOVERED.** Profile `ad8112ec` expires **2026-10-04T14:28:16Z**, and when it does **the app stops launching in his hand** — measured on 2026-09-27, when he said *"I can't open the app in my phone, looks like the profile already expired"* within minutes of the moment. ⚠️⚠️ **THE TWO THINGS THAT MUST BE TRUE BEFORE A RENEWAL CAN WORK, BOTH LEARNED THE EXPENSIVE WAY ON 2026-09-27:** **(1) the build must run ON or AFTER the expiry** — `-allowProvisioningUpdates` REUSES a valid profile and RENEWS only an expired or missing one, which seven early re-deploys demonstrated; and **(2) an Apple ID must be signed into Xcode** (*Xcode → Settings → Accounts*), because renewing talks to Apple and reusing does not. **Check (2) BEFORE spending ten minutes on a build**: `ls ~/Library/Developer/Xcode/UserData/Provisioning\ Profiles/ \| wc -l` and `security find-identity -v -p codesigning`. ⚠️ **AND THE OWNER MUST TAP TRUST AFTERWARDS, EVERY TIME** — *Settings → General → VPN & Device Management* — because a new PROFILE requires it even when the certificate is unchanged. That is the 2026-09-27 correction and it is not optional. ⚠️ **A free personal team signs for seven days**, so this row will have a successor, and the 2026-10-13 reading below needs the last one to land inside the seven days ending on that date. ⚠️⚠️ **AND A THIRD PREREQUISITE WAS LEARNED ON 2026-09-27 BY `6a-ii-a`, WHICH BUILT SUCCESSFULLY AND COULD NOT INSTALL: THE PHONE HAS TO BE REACHABLE, AND THE ERROR FOR *not reachable* IS NOT THE ERROR FOR *locked*.** `devicectl` reads `unavailable` and every install answers **`com.apple.dt.CoreDeviceError 1011`** — *unable to locate a device matching the requested device identifier* — where the passcode answers `kAMDMobileImageMounterDeviceLocked` or `CoreDeviceError 3`. **A retry loop written for the lock spins on `1011` for ever**, which is what twenty attempts over five minutes demonstrated. ⚠️ **Check it BEFORE spending ten minutes on a build**: `xcrun devicectl list devices` must read `available (paired)`, and `ping iPhone-de-Bernie.coredevice.local` must answer | ⚠️⚠️ **THE APP STOPS LAUNCHING WHEN IT PASSES, AND HE IS USING IT DAILY.** ⚠️ The data container survives a re-install — proved three times — so the cost is the trust tap and ten minutes, not his session | ☑ **DONE LATE, 2026-10-09** — missed on the day (the `9i` session found the profile expired and not renewed). Re-deployed 2026-10-09 **as `mx.wera.app`**, `5R-a`'s id, after the owner confirmed nothing was recorded offline before the old build stopped opening. `app/ios` was regenerated by `expo prebuild --clean`. ✅ **The embedded profile now expires 2027-10-09**: it was signed by the paid individual account (team `RGWA74UM3G`), so **the seven-day re-deploy is retired** and this row has no successor. It installed over WiFi and launched without a Trust prompt. The old `mx.bserafin.wera` stays on the phone as a dead icon until he deletes it |
| **2026-10-13** | ⚠️⚠️ **`5a-iv-d` day-30 reading — ON ONE INSTRUMENT NOW, NOT TWO, AND THE OWNER TRADED THE OTHER ON 2026-09-22 KNOWING WHAT IT COST.** ***"Override anything related to the 30 day session check. I want to keep working from my device."*** **The iPhone half is spent**: he is using the app daily, so its session is refreshed on every open and it can no longer answer *how long does a session survive untouched*. ⚠️ **The reading is still worth taking and still free, because the second instrument was sealed for exactly this**: the AVD `wera-reading-5a-iv-d`, a **different Google account**, no provisioning clock, powered down since 2026-09-21 — which is why a second instrument existed at all. **A one-device reading is a weaker claim than a two-device one, and it is the claim this project now has.** ⚠️ The iPhone row above is no longer part of this measurement. ⚠️⚠️ **AND IT NO LONGER MEASURES THIRTY DAYS — SAY SO NOW RATHER THAN DISCOVER IT THEN.** The day-8 reading of 2026-09-21 refreshed both sessions by opening both apps, which is what that reading IS; so this one measures **22 days** from 2026-09-21, not 30 from day 0. ⚠️ **That is not a defect and the row is still worth taking** — C1.4's claim is unbounded, and 22 untouched days is a longer gap than 8 — but *"day 30"* is now a name rather than an interval, and a session reporting it as thirty days since sign-in would be stating something false | C1.4 claims persistence *"until an explicit log-out"*, which is unbounded; **eight days can only fail to disprove it.** Both devices are sealed already, so this costs a glance. ⚠️ **The iPhone half also needs the 2026-09-27 re-deploy to have been TRUSTED** — see the row above. ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE ANDROID HALF MAY REFUSE TO BOOT: the sealed AVD was hard-killed on 2026-09-21 and left `hardware-qemu.ini.lock` and `multiinstance.lock` behind** in `~/.android/avd/wera-reading-5a-iv-d.avd/`. A stale lock reads as *"another emulator instance is running"* on the next boot — **which is this date, the one day it is expensive.** ✅ **The fix is to delete those two files and boot again**; the app's data is on the disk image and is not affected. ⚠️ **Do NOT launch Wera to check — booting the emulator is safe, opening the app is the reading** ⚠️⚠️ **2026-10-13, 01:52 UTC (the `5R-d-ii` session): both stale locks were DELETED; the boot itself was REFUSED by the session's permission check, so the owner runs it.** **Boot it COLD — `emulator -avd wera-reading-5a-iv-d -no-snapshot-load -no-snapshot-save`.** Its quick-boot snapshot (`snapshots/default_boot`) is the 2026-09-13 seal: loading it could roll the disk back to the day-0 refresh token, which the day-8 reading already spent, and with reuse detection on that replay revokes the session — a false red that looks exactly like the answer. Its `ram.bin` is 0 bytes (the 09-21 hard kill), so it may not load at all, which is not a reason to risk it | ☑ **DONE 2026-10-13 04:36 UTC — THE SESSION SURVIVED.** The owner booted it cold from Terminal.app (the flags above). Before reading the screen, the instrument was checked over `adb`: the AVD named `wera-reading-5a-iv-d`, its clock equal to the host's to the second (`auto_time` 1), `mx.bserafin.wera` the top resumed activity. **Wera opened signed in, on Inicio** — *Cerrar sesión* and the shop's $11.60 on screen (`uiautomator` and a screencap). The disk image was last written 2026-09-21 11:54 CST, so the session sat untouched **about 21½ days** and was restored by opening the app alone. ⚠️ **A weaker reading than day 8's**: a screen, not the before/after diff of `last_sign_in_at`, because that reads the stored credentials. **No successor is scheduled**; the AVD is free |
| **2027-04-30** | **Delete every pilot reading on the hosted project** (`delete from public.pilot_reading`, run by the owner: writes to the hosted database are his). **The aviso promises it** (`site/privacidad/`, section 6, and the deletion page), because a reading outlives the account it names (`5R-d-i`'s rehearsal measured it) and nothing else ever deletes one but the shop's own deletion. If the pilot runs longer, change the date on both pages **and announce it** before this day, as the aviso's section 9 says | A promise in a published legal notice, about data on named people; past the date it is simply false | ☐ |

▶️ **2026-10-22 — `8g`: *CONOCE WERA*, HIS TWO FIXES — THE BAR FILLS WHERE HE CAN SEE IT, AND A CARD STAYS AS LONG AS ITS WORDS TAKE. `8g` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK.**
**His words:** ***"The time bar at the top is not moving so people don't know how much time left they have on each slide. Also make the time longer on each slide, it's too little to understand what the screen is about and read. Fix the whole thing and let me know when we have our final version for both Android and iOS."***
**The cause of the first:** the bar WAS filling — the whole current segment was drawn at 35 % opacity and its fill sat inside that dimming, the same white as its own track, so it filled unseen. Now the dim track is its own layer behind a full-white fill, and the bar is half again as thick. **The second:** `CARD_MS` (6.5 s) is gone; `cardMs` (`@/navigation/recorrido`, tested) gives each card 2 s + 0.38 s a word + 4 s for its screen, floored at 8 s and capped at 20 s — the hello card 8.1 s, the screen cards 12.5 to 16.6 s, about a minute and a half for the owner's eight; a tap still moves on, a held finger holds. The length is read when a card starts (the store's address answers late and must not restart a card).
**Measured, both platforms, against the local verdulería:** the Android emulator, raw screenshots read pixel by pixel — card 3's bar from nothing to a quarter in about 3 s, card 4's from 27 to 65 to 95 of its 117 px at 4-second steps (≈15 s, the jitomate card's 15.1); the iPhone simulator, a Release build of `ebd6c5c` — card 1's bar at 59, 94, 129, 164 px every 2 s (≈8 s), then card 2's starting. **2281** tests (4 new), the gate **18 groups**.
**Decided on his behalf:** the reading rate (about two and a half words a second, for older customers — C3.18) and the floor and ceiling; *"Conoce Wera · 1 minuto"*, *"En un minuto…"* and Ajustes' *"en un minuto"* kept although the whole story now runs about a minute and a half — said in Mexico as *un minutito*, and most people tap ahead.
**Merged and shipped to `preview`:** `04c99e2`, pull request 397 — both jobs that could fire read by name, every step a success (**2281** tests, the gate **18 groups**, the rehearsal **10 cases** and **4 of 4**). iOS group `a5a8c064` on `d308ba9f…`, Android group `3a4903a0` on `5a4a7a5d…`.

**Looked at, both platforms, against the local shop:** Android emulator — an older shop got the card and no story; the card opened the 8 cards; the verdulería's owner got the story **by itself**; an Empleado got the 4 cards by herself; *Ajustes → Ver el recorrido* opened it; the last card's *Ir a Vender* landed on Vender. Found and fixed on the emulator: a tap left of the picture went forward (`locationX` is the touched child's), and the Empleado's last card had no way out. iPhone simulator (Release build of the commit) — opened by itself, the finger and the basket loop. Runtime unchanged: iOS `d308ba9f…`, Android `5a4a7a5d…`. ⚠️ The emulator's system hung (*"Process system isn't responding"*, up since Thursday) and Metro had stopped watching files: both restarted.

The pilot Android still owes it. Read the same night: his two shops' stores have complete addresses, so Merma opens for them; a third shop on the hosted project, *PerfectGonzo*, has none and its member will be asked on opening Merma.
**The iOS store build needs him once:** EAS cannot make the App Store distribution profile without a terminal (*"Distribution Certificate is not validated for non-interactive builds"*) — `cd ~/RetailerToolDev/RetailerManagementTool/app && npx eas-cli build --profile production --platform ios` in Terminal.app.

▶️ **2026-10-21 — THE FIRST DAY, WITH HIM: WALKED FIVE WAYS; RULED — THE TWO DOORS, TU TIENDA ASKS ONLY THE NAME AND THE GIROS, AND A CASHIER WHO JOINS LANDS ON VENDER. `8g` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK — the interview goes on (what to ask now and later, how someone joining finds her way, what to drop), then the rounds.**
**His words:** ***"We will do the 2 door version."*** — over the session's question 1 (https://claude.ai/artifact/DyN8SjULFMpdCbwCDqnH7U: today's order, two doors, or the shop first), the session's recommendation. With two notes: ***"We already have a logo, can you prepare an animation when opening the app for the first time with our cow that turns into the logo in the middle of the screen as you're showing me in the artifact, not that 'W' letter."*** and ***"Make the overall interface more beautiful, you are using the Letra Grande display as far as I can see. You can use regular sizes and more beautiful styling."*** (Measured: the emulator is at font scale 1.0 and density *Normal*; the first-day screens read as large because every label is body size in black boxes, so the note is about their styling.)
**The ruling:** the first screen asks whether she opens a shop or was invited (*Abrir mi tienda* · *Me invitaron a una tienda* · *¿Ya tienes cuenta? Entrar*); then her account, the same for both; then only her half. JavaScript only — a new first screen, the door remembered on the phone through the account step.
**The estimate, written first:** the walk and the interview `M`; the build is sized once the path is agreed.
**The walk** (https://claude.ai/artifact/AjFfTqb9A46meAbwWMsYsb — every screen, markable): the Android emulator against the local shop, a fresh install for each path, every tap logged as made. To the shop's Inicio: **an owner by email 14 taps · 9 boxes · 1 scroll** (+3 taps and the slide to the first sale); **invited by email 9 · 5 · 2**; **the shop's code 9 · 5 · 2**, plus leaving Wera and coming back after the approval. The owner: inviting by email 6 taps and 1 box, sharing the code 2, approving 3. **No signal at the start:** neither the account nor the shop can be made — *Sin conexión* after everything is typed (3 s, 5 s), the form kept, and the retry made one shop. Google and Apple are off in the local stack, so that path stops at the hand-off. The iPhone 17 simulator (a Release build from a worktree, `.claude/worktrees/ios-sim`) showed Entrar and Tu tienda; it takes no taps.
**Two defects measured, not yet fixed** (his: no change until the path is agreed): ⚠️ **(1) after joining a shop, Vender says *Todavía no hay productos.*** — Rosa (invitation) and Pepe (code), each in a shop of 841, until a return to the app past five minutes: `useStarterCatalog` reads `CATALOG_KEY` on Tu tienda while she belongs to no shop, `useOnboardWorkspace` invalidates it and joining does not. ⚠️ **(2) *"Cuando te acepten, la tienda se abre sola aquí"* is false while she watches:** nothing re-reads the membership while a request is pending — approved at 18:55:26, Pepe's screen unmoved 2½ minutes later, open 6 s after he left Wera and came back.
**Also seen, for the interview:** the invitation message carries no way to get Wera; someone invited who signs up by email and types the shop's code loses her invitation and waits (`0055`, known); the giro she chose vanishes from the grid while the shop is made; the genders are fixed (*Dueño* for Ana, *dejarla entrar* for Lalo); scrolled text runs under the clock on Tu tienda; Apple's button speaks the phone's language. Read for question 2: the address columns are nullable (`0058`) and `onboard_workspace` never takes them; all 1,161 catalog products are at 0% IVA; ADR-035 §2.5 puts the IVA question at setup.
**Merged on the way:** pull request 375 (`da12ca0`, the setup) — read by name: typecheck, Vitest **2249 in 80 files**, the gate 18 of 18 and its 36 fixtures, the runbook's 10 cases and 4 broken copies, the documents agreeing. Nothing undeployed: the hosted project holds `0001`–`0068`.
**His second ruling, question 2:** ***"Let's do Option B. In the ¿Qué vendes? question you can add the legend 'Puedes escoger mas de un giro, el primero que escojas es tu giro principal.' and 'Te ponemos sus productos, con precio recomendado'. Omit the ¿Tus precios ya incluyen IVA? question for this point we'll assume Si, ya lo incluyen for everyone, changeable in the settings."*** — so Tu tienda asks **the name and the giros** (with his two lines), **the address moves to Te lo compro**, the one screen that uses it (the columns are nullable; Te lo compro already handles a shop without one), and **IVA is not asked**: every shop starts at *Sí* (`prices_include_tax`'s default) and the owner changes it in Ajustes (`workspace` update is granted and owner-only since `0001`, so JavaScript only). ⚠️ **Over the session's recommendation, which kept the IVA question because ADR-035 §2.5 put it at setup — the ADR now says his ruling.** ⚠️ A change of the setting re-reads every stored price (gross while *Sí*, net while *No*); at 0%, every catalog product, nothing moves.
**His third ruling, question 3:** ***"Let's do B, the message wording is fine. The address should be requested when opening Merma, not when hitting Te lo compro."*** — so someone joining as an **Empleado lands on Vender**, with *Ya estás en Abarrotes Ana* said once; an **Encargado lands on Inicio**, as the owner does. Fixed with it, as the walk's findings: the share message names the door (*Te invité a Abarrotes Ana en Wera. Abre Wera, toca «Me invitaron a una tienda» y escribe este código: …*); the waiting screen re-reads the membership every few seconds, so it does open by itself, and carries *Avisar por WhatsApp*; Vender is not empty after joining. **And the address moves again: asked when Merma is opened**, not at Te lo compro (who is asked, and whether it can wait, is in question 4).
**His fourth ruling, question 4, row by row:** ***"1. Don't make it dodgeable if a person wants to log Merma. 2. Encargado should also be enrollable via whatsapp 3. Follow your recommendation. 4. Your recommendation. 5. Follow your recommendation 6. Keep it 7. Keep it, quiet. 8. Optional is fine."*** — so: (1) **the address cannot be put off**: no *Ahora no*; the owner gives it before she logs merma. (3) **The owner lands on Vender after *Abrir mi tienda***, told *Tu tienda está lista* once. (4) The second tap to let someone in stays, as *Sí, dejar entrar*. (5) Role words stay (*Dueño*); verbs and sentences around them lose their gender. (6) Apellido stays required. (7) *Cerrar sesión* and *Eliminar mi cuenta* stay, quiet, before a shop exists. (8) Colonia stays optional.
⚠️ **Two follow-ups asked and not yet answered** (each needs his word, and two of the three answers are a migration): **(1′)** only the owner can save the address (`location_update`, owner-only since `0001`) — so when an Encargado or Empleado opens Merma in a shop with none, do they pass (the session's recommendation; no database change), must whoever comes first fill an empty address (a migration: a narrow function), or wait for the owner? **(2′)** an Encargado *"enrollable via whatsapp"*: the role chosen at approval, so everyone joins with the shop's code and the email invitation goes (the session's recommendation; a migration — `approve_request` gains the role), an invitation without an email (a migration), or today's email invitation, whose code already goes out by WhatsApp (nothing to build)?
**The estimate, written now the path is agreed:** `XL`, so it splits on size into three rounds, each shipped to his iPhone on its own. **Round 1 (`M`):** the two defects (Vender after joining; the waiting screen opening by itself, with *Avisar por WhatsApp*), the share messages naming the door, the genders, and the arrivals (an Empleado and a new owner land on Vender with one line; an Encargado on Inicio). **Round 2 (`L`):** the two doors — the first screen with the cow turning into the logo, the account screen, Tu tienda with the name and the giros and his two lines, *Entrar a la tienda*, in Wera's styling at regular sizes. **Round 3 (`M`):** IVA in Ajustes; Merma's address, not skippable; the Encargado — after (1′) and (2′).
**Round 1, built:** `@/auth/guard` — leaving the landing is arriving: `redirectFor` reads her role there and only there (an owner and an Empleado to `/vender`, an Encargado to `/`, nobody moved until the roster says); `@/auth/llegada` (new, pure) — *Tu tienda está lista* for the owner, *Ya estás en {tienda}* for the Empleado, nothing for an Encargado, taken once; the gate announces it, restores no earlier screen over an arrival, and **re-reads every query the moment the membership turns `member`**; `useStarterCatalog` reads the shop's catalog only once there is a shop (the root of the empty Vender); `useWaitForApproval` re-reads the membership and her requests every 5 s while one is pending; *Avisar por WhatsApp* under it (`whatsappChooseUrl`, no number: WhatsApp asks to whom); Vender shows the arrival as its confirmation, held as long as a note (`Hecho`'s `lingers`); *Sí, dejar entrar* and *…aparece aquí y tú decides si entra.* The share messages wait for round 2: they name a door that exists only then.
**Measured on the Android emulator** (local shop, fresh installs): a new owner's *Crear mi tienda* → Vender, Frutería's products, *Tu tienda está lista* 8 s after the tap; a cashier asking with the shop's code, approved through the API at 20:16:03 → **her screen moved by itself 7 s later**, to Vender with *Ya estás en Cremeria Olga* over the shop's products (before: unmoved for 2½ minutes, then an empty Vender); an Encargado redeeming an invitation → Inicio, no line; the owner's confirm step reads *Sí, dejar entrar*. Vitest **2255 in 81 files** (6 new: the arrival suite, the WhatsApp link, the guard's arrival rows), the gate 18 of 18. **Not measured:** iOS; the WhatsApp button's chooser (no WhatsApp on the emulator).
**Decided on his behalf:** the poll every 5 s; the WhatsApp text, *Hola, te pedí entrar a {tienda} en Wera. Ábrela y entra a Solicitudes para dejarme entrar.*; a roster that cannot be read sends her to Vender rather than holding her on the landing; the arrival line holds 4.5 s, as the first sale's does.
**Round 1 merged and shipped:** `0355712`, pull request 376 — 11 jobs, each read by name: Vitest **2255 in 81 files**, the gate 18 of 18 and its 36 fixtures, the runbook's 10 cases and 4 broken copies, the nine `db.yml` jobs. ⚠️ *The prebuilt catalog, its photos, and the delete* first failed at *Start Postgres* on Docker Hub's *toomanyrequests: Rate exceeded*, every later step skipped — a throttle, not an assertion; re-run, every step *success*, its four checks and their fixtures green. iOS group `439adc1b`, Android `a6757716`.
**Round 2, built — the two doors:** `(auth)/hola` (new) — the first screen: the two doors and *¿Ya tienes cuenta? Entrar* (`@/ui/Puertas`), and **the cow turning into the logo, once per phone**: the rust cow on the splash's own frame (`@/theme/apertura`, pinned to `app.json` and `render-icons.sh` by `app/test/apertura.test.ts`), the icon's rust square growing behind her, her turn to cream, a nod, the rise to the density's seal, the doors arriving (1.8 s; settled at once when the phone asks for less motion). `brand/render-vaca.sh` (new) draws the two pictures from `brand/wera-mark.svg`; the rust is `OXIDO_DE_LA_VACA`, beside the palette's roles and not one. `@/lib/splash` (new) holds the native splash until the screen behind it is hers — **measured: without it, Inicio flashed for a frame and the square grew empty while the pictures loaded** — and lets go by itself after 4 s whatever happens. `@/auth/guard` sends a stranger to `/hola`. `@/auth/puerta` (new) keeps her door on the phone through the account step. **The account** (`entrar.tsx`): the band with her door above *Tu cuenta*, *Continuar con Google* (and Apple's black button) first, *o con tu correo*, Nombre · Apellido side by side, each box's own key leading to the next (`@/ui/Campo`, `@/ui/Banda`, new). **Her half** (`bienvenida.tsx`): *Tu tienda* asks the name and the giros under his two lines, no IVA, no address, *Abrir mi tienda*; *Entrar a la tienda* is the one box; an account with no door sees the doors again; a pending request always sees its half. **IVA in Ajustes** (`Iva`, the owner's; `setPricesIncludeTax`). **Merma asks the owner for the address**, with no way past it, when the read says there is none (`PideDireccion`); offline she logs merma and is asked when the read returns; the others pass, pending (1′). The share messages name the door.
**Measured on the Android emulator** (local shop, fresh installs, the debug build): the opening in a burst of on-device frames — the welcome first, no Inicio flash, the cow cream on the square, the nod caught mid-rise, settled (⚠️ the first run showed the rust cow vanishing into the rust square before she turned: the cream now starts with the square); the owner by email **12 taps (5 of them the keyboard's own key), 5 boxes, no scroll, to Vender with *Tu tienda está lista*** (was 14 taps, 9 boxes, a scroll and six forced keyboard closes); Merma then asked the address and opened once it was saved (the row read back); Ajustes changed IVA to *No* and back, the row following; someone joining by the shop's code **10 taps, 5 boxes, no scroll** to the waiting card, and **12 s after the approval her screen opened by itself** on Vender with *Ya estás en Abarrotes Ines*. Vitest **2264 in 83 files**, the gate 18 of 18. **Not measured:** the iPhone (the simulator takes no taps; his phone is the instrument), Google and Apple (off locally), the release build's splash hand-off.
**Decided on his behalf:** Apple's button black, beside Google's white; the doors' icons (a shop, a key); *Wera* in Nunito under the logo (the lettering was never ruled); the settled logo at the density's seal size, a quarter of the way down; the opening's timings as the page he saw, with the cream moved earlier; the splash's 4 s safety; Merma not asking while the address read is out (offline); *Volver* from her half back to the doors.
**Round 2 merged and shipped:** `051b36c`, pull request 377 — 11 jobs, every step of each read by name *success*: Vitest **2264 in 83 files**, the gate 18 of 18 and its 36 fixtures, the runbook's 10 cases and 4 broken copies, the nine `db.yml` jobs. ⚠️ **The first publish sent nothing** (the channel's newest was still round 1's; its log was lost to a filter, so the cause is not known); the retry from the same clean `main` published both: iOS group `99487824`, Android `b9b685d3`.
**His rulings on the two follow-ups, the same night:** ***"1. No one can log Merma until they provide the address with CP, they can also set it in the Configuracion without opening Merma 2. Let's go with your recommendation, the owner can always change the role of someone though."*** — (1′) **every role is asked the address in Merma and cannot pass**, and may give it in Ajustes without opening Merma (over the session's recommendation that only the owner be asked): a migration, since only the owner may write a store today. (2′) **the role is chosen when she is let in**, everyone joins with the shop's code, the email invitation leaves Tu equipo, and **the owner can change anyone's role** afterwards. Both go to `8q`, round 3, unblocked; the decisions block is empty again. `8g` stays the next task: what he says of rounds 1 and 2 on his phones is its next round.
**Round 3 (`8q`), built the same night — the estimate, written in the row first: `L`, one migration.** `0069_store_address_by_anyone.sql` — `set_store_address(…)`, `security definer`: anyone the store is visible to (`my_locations()`) FILLS an address that is not complete; changing a complete one stays the owner's (`TD012`), a missing field is `22023`; `location_update` (owner-only) is untouched. Its suite, `supabase/tests/0069_store_address_by_anyone.sql`, **11 checks under `set role authenticated`**: a cashier fills an empty one; she and an Encargado cannot change a complete one; the owner can; another shop's owner cannot; a missing or malformed código postal writes nothing; a cashier's direct update of the table changes nothing. The app writes every address through it (`SET_STORE_ADDRESS`, `setStoreAddressArgs`; `TD012` → *La tienda ya tiene dirección. Solo el dueño puede cambiarla.*); **Merma asks every role** and lets nobody past; Ajustes offers the address to anyone while it is missing. **The role:** Solicitudes asks *¿Qué va a poder hacer?* (Empleado · Encargado) as she is let in — admitted as she asked, then made Encargado by the owner's update of her membership row (`MEMBER_TABLE`, `MEMBER_ROLE_COLUMN`; owner-only since `0001`, so no migration); Tu equipo's rows carry *Cambiar* for the owner (Empleado ↔ Encargado), and one made Empleado is given the shop's stores; **the email invitation (`Invitar`) is gone from Tu equipo** — the shop's code is the one way in. Contracts: `8k-collection-contract.sh` writes the address through the function by the app's names (15 groups; its falsifier 7 fixtures, a renamed argument new); `5b-iii-d-2-approve-contract.sh` gains the owner's role change (she then reads both stores) and a manager's refused one (16 groups; its falsifier **19 fixtures**, two new).
**Measured on the Android emulator** (local shop, `0069` applied by hand): Tere, an Empleado, opened Merma in a shop whose address was emptied — asked, filled it, Merma opened on Tirar; the row read *Bolivar 8, 06000*; her Ajustes shows it with no *Cambiar*. Olga, signed in through *¿Ya tienes cuenta? Entrar*: Solicitudes → *Dejar entrar* → *Encargado* → *Sí, dejar entrar*: Memo became `manager`; Tu equipo → Pepa (an Encargado by email, no store) → *Empleado*: `staff`, one store given; Beto → *Encargado*: `manager`; *Invitar a alguien* absent. **Not measured:** iOS; the store assignment on a demotion over HTTP (the emulator and the database read it back; no contract).
**Decided on his behalf:** a filled address is changed by the owner only (his *"they can also set it"* read as giving a missing one); offline, Merma waits for the address read rather than refusing (it is not kept on the phone); the role is chosen after the request, as two writes — if the second fails she stays Empleado, which Tu equipo fixes; the email invitation's code paths stay in the database, unused by the app; the owner's own row and other owners carry no *Cambiar*.
**Order, owed after the merge:** `supabase db push` and a green `5R-f-schema-deployed.sh` **before** the update ships — a phone calling `set_store_address` on a database without it is a 404 on every address.
**Round 3 merged, deployed and shipped:** `0cd05a6`, pull request 379 — 11 jobs, every step of each *success*: `0069`'s suite *all 11 checks passed*, `8k`'s contract 15 groups and its falsifier 7 fixtures, the approval contract 16 groups and its falsifier 19, Vitest **2265 in 83 files**, the gate 18 of 18 and its 36 fixtures. **Deployed in order:** `supabase db push` applied `0069` (the dry run named it alone); `5R-f` green, 67 migrations on both sides; read off the hosted project, `set_store_address(p_location_id uuid, p_street text, p_street_number text, p_colonia text, p_city text, p_postal_code text)`, executable by `authenticated` and not by `anon`. **Then** the update: iOS group `ca1f9451`, Android `948b6e50`.
**Next:** his look at rounds 1–3 on the iPhone.

✅ **2026-10-21 — THE FIRST DAY IS SET UP FOR THE NEXT SESSION. `8g` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK — with him: the first day. He pastes *The First-Day Prompt* (`docs/HANDBOOK.md`, *How a working session goes*).**
**His words:** ***"Set everything up for us to go through first day in the next session. I want to explore the different options, what should go first, creating an account, giving option to open a store, I want to go through your suggestions through on the different paths and to onboard the people as easy and quick as possible. I want to clear context and start this in a new session."***
**Where the first day stands — read off the code that night; nobody has walked it end to end since `8g` began, so the next session's first job is to:**
1. ***Entrar*** (`app/src/app/(auth)/entrar.tsx`): email and password, *Entrar con Google*, *Entrar con Apple* on iPhone (`5R-k`). *Crear una* switches to sign-up: name, surname, email, password and the aviso's tick (`5R-d-iii`; the name is asked once, here — `5b.7`). *¿Olvidaste tu contraseña?* opens WhatsApp to the owner's support number (`5R-h`) — the hosted project has autoconfirm on and no SMTP, so no reset email exists.
2. ***Bienvenida*** (`app/src/app/(onboarding)/bienvenida.tsx`), where `@/auth/guard`'s `redirectFor` sends a signed-in person with no shop: **Tu tienda** — *¿Cómo se llama tu tienda?*, *¿Tus precios ya incluyen IVA?*, the giros with *Tu giro principal*, *¿Dónde está tu tienda?* (calle, número, colonia opcional, ciudad o alcaldía, código postal) — then *Crear mi tienda*, which imports the giros' starter catalog with no list to untick (ruled 2026-10-14) and prices it (`0067`). Below a rule, **¿Te invitaron?**: one box that decides by length — 8 characters is the shop's code (a request the owner approves in *Solicitudes*), 16 is an invitation (`P2`, ADR-035 §2.7 D7). *Cerrar sesión* and account deletion sit there too.
3. Then the tabs, on Inicio. The first sale carries *Wera funciona sin internet…* once; the first outage, *Sin internet: puedes seguir vendiendo* (round 3).
**What earlier rulings already fix — grep the corpus before proposing against them:** the aviso's tick at sign-up; the name asked once; one box for both codes, the joining half below the creating half; no catalog list at onboarding (2026-10-14); someone invited is simply let in (D7), with the question found 2026-10-13 — *an invitation is taken by whoever signs up with that address* — in the archived decisions; `onboard_workspace` has no idempotency key, so a lost reply can make two shops (since 2026-10-20 a slow one can end in the time limit too).
**What the session brings him first:** today's first day walked on the Android emulator (and the iPhone simulator if it builds) against the local test shop with a brand-new account, every screen captured and every tap and field counted, for each path — an owner opening a shop; a cashier joining with the shop's code; someone invited by email; Google or Apple instead of email; a phone with no signal at the start — on one page he can mark up; then the interview, one question at a time, each with a recommendation. ⚠️ **One-way doors here:** anything `onboard_workspace` or the seed bakes in, a migration, a new build (Google/Apple sign-in are native), a change to who may join a shop or how — his to decide first.
**Merged and shipped since the last entries:** pull request 373 (`5c87de5`, the time limit) — iOS group `e844fdbd`, Android `bd889e4b`; pull request 374 (`4e8acfa`, *Agregar del catálogo*'s line) — 11 jobs read by name, Vitest **2249 in 80 files**, the session reading 8 groups and 9 fixtures — iOS group `c32dbb03`, Android `cd0172b8`.

✅ **2026-10-21, just after midnight — *AGREGAR DEL CATÁLOGO* SAYS WHAT IT DID NOT ADD, AND WHY, IN ONE LINE. `8g` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK — with him: the first day, the last thing before the Android users.**
**His words:** ***"In my iphone I'm on the Productos Screen, and clicked on Agregar del Catalogo to add 17 products from Polleria but I'm only seeing 6 products in that category. What's going wrong?"*** — then, over the session's question, ***"Say so, in one line"***.
**What was wrong, read off the hosted database** (reads only): no product was created in any shop that day. In *Pollería y Recaudería Bernabe* the catalog's Pollería set is 59 products, 42 held and **17 missing — the 17 he chose, all of the catalog's *Pollo* family, all sold by weight.** His shop's *Pollo* aisle holds five products by weight and **Pollo entero by the piece**, imported on 2026-09-22 from a catalog that then sold it by the piece (today's sells it by weight; 1 sale and 1 price recorded against it). `import_catalog` (0067) never puts a product into an aisle that holds another measure (C8.5), so it skipped all 17 — and `catalogo.tsx` ignored the answer and went back, so they looked added. ⚠️ Not a defect in the catalog: its *Pollo* family is one measure; the other two shops simply never imported it.
**What changed:** `notAddedOf` and `aislesFrom` in `@/api/starterCatalog` — the count is the server's (*offered minus imported*; `skipped` cannot be used, the whole giro is sent so it counts what the shop already holds), the aisles and their measures are the shop's own catalog read after the import, and when that read does not account for the count the line names no aisle rather than a wrong one; `useStarterCatalog` returns the aisles; *Agregar del catálogo* goes back only when everything arrived, and otherwise stays with *No agregamos 17 productos de Pollo: ese pasillo de tu tienda mezcla productos por pieza y por peso.* (`ES.starter.notAdded`, `notAddedSome`, `measure`). No database change.
**Measured on the Android emulator** (the local shop, given a *Pollo* aisle holding *Pollo rostizado* by the piece, removed afterwards): Pollería → *Agregar 22 productos* → the screen stayed and read *No agregamos 22 productos de Pollo: ese pasillo de tu tienda ya tiene productos por pieza.*; the database confirmed none was created.
**Decided on his behalf:** the line's wording, *por pieza / por peso / por litro*; the screen stays when anything did not arrive.
**His shop, fixed on his word** (*"Pollo entero to its own aisle"*, over the session's recommendation): in one transaction on the hosted database — a new aisle *Pollo entero*, his *Pollo entero* by the piece moved into it (the transaction refused to commit unless exactly one row moved; its sale and price untouched), and `import_catalog(…, '{giro-polleria}')` run as the shop's owner: **`{"imported": 17, "priced": 17, "skipped": 42}`**. Read back: Pollería **59 of 59** held, *Pollo* 22 products all by weight, *Pollo entero* 1 by the piece.

✅ **2026-10-20, night — EVERY REQUEST HAS A TIME LIMIT: 30 s, 60 s FOR THE CATALOG, 90 s FOR A PHOTO GOING UP. `8g` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK — with him: the first day, the last thing before the Android users.**
**His words:** ***"Go ahead with the request time limit before the Android users."***
**The estimate, written first:** `S` — one pure module and one line in the client. Read on the way in: React Native's `OkHttpClientProvider` builds Android's client with every timeout at zero; every request of the app's one client goes through its `fetch`, and supabase-js hands that `fetch` to the database, storage, the functions and the sign-in's renewal alike; photo downloads go through Expo's downloader, which has OkHttp's own 10 s defaults. **The limits are measured:** the largest answer the app receives is the catalog, **382 KB** for 841 products (read off the local gateway's log), about 31 s at 100 kbps — 60 s, with the catalog import; a photo is 1280 px at JPEG 0.7, 150–300 KB, and goes up slower — 90 s; everything else is a few KB — 30 s.
**What changed:** `@/lib/plazo` — `withLimit` (an `AbortController` per request, a caller's own signal still honoured, the timer cleared when the request settles), `limitFor` by path and method, and a request over its limit fails as an `AbortError` whose message says `Network request failed`, so `@/api/errors` and `@/auth/errors` read it as *Sin conexión*, `auth-js` keeps the session, the outbox retries it and Te lo compro holds its send; `@/lib/supabase` — `global: { fetch: withLimit(fetch) }`; `@/api/QueryProvider` — a read that ran out of time is not retried (`isTimedOut`).
**Measured on the Android emulator, with the local gateway paused so requests are accepted and never answered:** with a temporary log in the wrapper (removed before the commit), every request passed through it, the limit fired at **exactly 30 s** and the request was cancelled (*Fetch request has been canceled*). ⚠️⚠️ **And the first version was not enough:** failing as a `TypeError`, postgrest-js retried the read three more times by itself (1, 2, 4 s apart, 30 s each) and the data library retried that chain twice — a hung read took about **six minutes** to say so. Named `AbortError` — which postgrest-js never retries — and not retried by the data library, **Precios read *Sin conexión a internet. Intenta de nuevo en un momento.* at 38 s.** A sale rung up while the gateway hung landed 5 s after it returned. ⚠️ **What this could not show:** a link that drops packets silently — the hang behind the evening's stuck Te lo compro send — needs a firewall rule this machine does not allow; cutting the tunnel closes the connection instead, and the old client then fails at once too (measured: both landed 11 s after it returned). **Not measured:** iOS, whose own client has a 60 s default.
**Decided on his behalf:** the three limits above; a read that ran out of time is said at once and read again by itself on the reconnect, the return and the pull, rather than tried twice more; a write that ran out of time may still have landed — the outbox's writes and Te lo compro's are idempotent by their client ids, and creating a shop (`onboard_workspace`, no idempotency key) is the one write where a slow-but-landed answer followed by a second tap could make two, as a lost reply already could.

✅ **2026-10-19, late night — `8p` IS BUILT: A CORRECTION COUNTS ON THE DAY OF WHAT IT CORRECTS (`0064`). `8g` IS THE NEXT TASK, WITH HIM.**
**Estimate, written before building:** `L`, bordering `XL` — one migration over three write functions and two views, the offline queue's payload, four contracts; one sitting, because the view half alone would fix deletions and leave corrections moving to today. **What the estimate missed:** the seed checks. Six of `supabase/checks/` pin numbers computed under the old rule (the seed holds three deliveries voided on a later day — 23 lines — and one write-off), and one of them (`0031`) holds `product_purchases_daily` equal, row for row, to the copy inside `product_waste_daily` — so the rule had to move in **all four** daily views or two of them would disagree. Still one sitting.
**What changed — the database (`0064`):** `record_sale`, `record_purchase` and `record_waste` take **`p_corrects`** (dropped and created, `0025`'s decision 6; bodies copied from `0025` and `0057` by a script that asserts the only differences are the argument and one branch). When it names a document **of this store, standing reversed, reversed by the caller**, the new document takes that document's `occurred_at` from the ledger, at any age; anything else falls through to the old rules — never an error. Grants re-issued. **Every daily view** — `product_velocity_daily`, `product_purchases_daily`, `product_margin_daily`, `product_waste_daily` (inside `0040`'s fence) — counts a reversal on its original's day and orders a day's last price by that time; their stale comments re-issued. The ledger's rows and `transaction_export` keep when each void was made. ⚠️ **On Precios this means a cancelled delivery is no longer a point on the cost chart** — its day nets to zero — and a corrected one shows the re-keyed price; `docs/checks/7b-prices-contract.sh` asserted the old negative day and was restated (11 groups, its 6 fixtures red for their reasons) after CI's first run caught it — the one contract the session's local pass had not run.
**What changed — the app:** *Hoy* (`takingsFrom`) skips a void whose sale is not in today's window. `prefillOf` names the voided document (`corrects`: its id and its time) and, for a write-off, its day (`wasteDay`); the cart store keeps the link per basket and drops it with the basket (*Vaciar*, the last line leaving, a commit, another shop, another load) or when she picks another day on a corrected write-off; `draftOf` sends it as `corrects` + the original's `occurred_at`, which `@/api/flush` turns into `p_corrects`. An unsent note corrects nothing (`unsentPrefillOf`).
**Evidence — the schema (local, from a clean `supabase db reset`):** every seed check — `0009` 36, `0011` 57, `0012` 35, `0013` 49, `0014` 30, `0015` 10, `0017` 9, `0031` 48, `0032` 48, `0033` 43, `seed_invariant` 18 — and **all 43 behavioural suites**, the new `0064` suite's 22 among them; that suite goes red (2.1, 2.3, 2.4, 4.1) with the branch taken out of `record_sale`. **The code:** Vitest **2004 passed, 67 files**; the gate 18 of 18; `5h-ii-b`'s contract **26 groups** over a real PostgREST (a sale from yesterday, deleted and re-keyed online, lands on yesterday at its own instant; today's stays today; the void keeps its moment) and its falsifier **11 fixtures**, the two new ones red for their reasons. **On the Android emulator, Luz's local shop** (rebuilt; see below): *HOY $110 · 2 ventas*; yesterday's $45 sale deleted from Lo último — the ledger's −$45 is stamped today 17:05 and *HOY* still reads **$110 · 2 ventas** (it read −$45 · 0 ventas this morning); yesterday's $40 Concha corrected to 3 from Lo último and slid through Vender — it landed at **2026-10-18 16:07:07.523955, the original's instant**, recorded today 17:08; Lo último *AYER · 1 VENTA $30* at 16:07; Números *Día*: today $110 with no negative line, *18 de octubre* $30; the export files each void on the 19th and the corrected sale on the 18th. ⚠️ **Found, not caused, not fixed:** the first correction tried opened Vender EMPTY — the phone's saved cart belonged to the shop that existed before the reset, and Vender's `openShop` empties another shop's baskets, wiping the prefill just loaded (the void stood, so it acted as a deletion). Only the first capture-screen visit after a phone changes shops; the pilot shops each have one. ⚠️ **And a local loss of mine:** running the suites locally truncated the local shop (`_cleanup.sql` empties every table); Luz's shop was rebuilt over the API for this measurement, without the photos.
**Decided on his behalf:** ⚠️⚠️ **FLAGGED — CHEAP NOW, A MIGRATION LATER:** (1) **all four daily views** follow the rule, not the two the screens read today — so no future screen can disagree about a day; (2) **the link only matches a void made by the same person** (and the same store) — narrower than the row's *"same shop and already voided"*: nobody can place a document anywhere they could not already have undone one, and a mismatch lands the old way, silently. Cheap: (3) `transaction_export` unchanged — a deletion stays on the day it was made there, as the ruling said (*our side keeps the real timeline*); `supabase/checks/0033` now pins exactly which rows it dates differently (26, all reversals); (4) a corrected write-off opens on its own day in *¿Cuándo lo tiraste?*, and picking another day is her answer (the link is dropped); older than *Antier* the chips show *Hoy* while it saves on its day — **left for `8g`'s look at Desperdicio**; (5) an unsent note re-keyed is a new write (it never reached the ledger).

**His ruling:** ***"Let's follow your recommendation."*** — the decisions block's question of the same evening; the block is empty again. **Sizing it found more than a view:** the server re-dates every ONLINE write to `now()` (`0025`), so putting a corrected document back at its original time needs the three `record_*` functions to accept the document it replaces. Who may read it is unchanged: `0040` lets every role read deliveries and their prices.
**Evidence:** Vitest **1946 passed, 64 files** (8 new: a drop per kilo times kilos, a rise, another supplier, earlier this week, before the week, a void, rows out of order, a price that will not parse, Monday at midnight, his sentence); the gate 18 of 18. `5g-iii`'s contract **14 groups**, 3 new for this read against a real PostgREST: the bound keeps exactly the lines on or after it and drops the two older; the quantity arrives as a string; two lines of one delivery come back in id order and pages of one add up to the whole. **Decided on his behalf:** *la vez pasada* is the previous delivery of that product **from the same supplier**, never the cheapest anywhere; the week runs Monday to Sunday on the phone's clock, as Números counts; a price that went UP subtracts nothing (the card counts what went well; a rise is Costos' to show); a voided delivery is neither a saving nor a previous price (Costos' rule); **the card is not drawn at $0**; every role sees it, as every role reads Costos (`0040`); it is not kept on the phone for offline, so with no signal at launch it is simply absent.

The worst case becomes one lost day for one shop, and a phone still holds what it has not sent. It needs an ADR amendment, because the ADR asks for more. **The other way** is the ADR as written: Pro and the add-on. ⚠️ Until you rule, a copy of the shop's rows as of 2026-10-13 is on the Mac in `~/RetailerToolDev/wera-backups/`: 22 tables read through the API. (1) ⚠️⚠️ **A manager can make an owner.** `create_invite` refuses a manager who asks for an owner invite. But the manager may INSERT the same row straight into `workspace_invite` (`0002`'s policy and grant, never withdrawn), and a second login that types the shop's code is then let in by `request_access` as OWNER, with no approval. The seed shop went from one owner to two. (2) **An owner can erase a membership row**, which skips the tombstone you ruled on 2026-10-09 (the shop keeps the name) and takes that person's pilot readings with it. `0053` left it *"parked for the owner"* in a comment, and it reached no list. > | **An invitation is taken by whoever signs up with that address. Ask for proof?** Found 2026-10-13. ADR-035 §2.7 D7: someone already invited who types the shop's code *"is simply let in"*. `request_access` matches the login's email and never asks whether it was confirmed (shown locally with a login that never confirmed), and on the live project an email sign-up needs no confirmation (your ruling of 2026-10-09; `5R-j` is parked). So anybody who knows a shop's code and an invited person's address can make that login first and join at the invited role. Every member can read the code, and it travels by WhatsApp. **Recommendation: D7 lets in only a Google sign-in, where Google has verified the address. An email-and-password login that types the code files an ordinary request, and the owner approves it by name.** It costs an invited person who signed up by email one tap from the owner. **The other way** is to bring email confirmation forward (`5R-j`), which needs a mail provider first. ⚠️ **Added by `5R-k-a`, 2026-10-13:** an Apple sign-in's address is verified by Apple as a Google one is by Google, so the recommendation reads *Google or Apple*; an address Apple invented matches no invitation and already arrives as an ordinary request. ⚠️ **And one more consequence of the same root, read and NOT measured:** Supabase joins a Google or Apple sign-in to an existing login with the same address when that login counts as confirmed, and with confirmation off every email sign-up counts. So a login made first, with somebody else's address and a password, could be the one their later Google or Apple sign-in lands in. Measure it when this question is taken | `5P-e` — the pilot's start list, before a shop invites anybody | This row |

**What the estimate found (`M` as written, `L` as built, and split — the reason is on the row):**
(1) **No Services ID is needed.** Supabase: *"If you're building a native app only, you do not need to configure the OAuth settings."* So nothing expires every six months, and Supabase needs only the provider on with `mx.wera.app` as a client id. The key is needed by the function alone.
(2) **The exchange is at the deletion, not at sign-in** as the row had it. Apple's code is good for five minutes and one use, so the phone asks Apple again at that moment and nothing is kept in between.
(3) **Which string goes where was read in the auth server's source**, not assumed: GoTrue's `internal/api/token_oidc.go` compares the SHA-256 of the nonce it is handed, as lower-case hex, with the nonce inside Apple's token. So Apple gets the hash and Supabase the raw string. The other way round typechecks and refuses every sign-in.
(4) **Apple sends the name on the first sign-in only, and never in the token.** The app writes it; a login that already has a name keeps it.
(5) **The gateway's token check would admit a deleted account** (it reads a signature only), so `verify_jwt` is off for this function and it asks the auth server itself. Measured: the still-signed token of a deleted account is refused.
(6) **An Edge Function runs wherever is nearest the phone**, and the aviso says the United States. The call is pinned to `us-west-2`, where the database is.
(7) **The two storage permissions are not Expo's template's**, as the row said: `expo-file-system`'s and `expo-image`'s own manifests declare them, up to Android 12. Both of Wera's share paths write to the app's own cache (`expo-print`'s `context.cacheDir`, `Paths.cache`), and `expo-sharing` treats that folder as readable without asking the system. ⚠️ **Read in the libraries' source, not run on Android 12**: this Mac has only an Android 16 image.
(8) **The capability changes the iPhone's signing profile**, so the iOS build needs an Apple login and is his, in Terminal.app.
(9) ⚠️ **An account made with Apple cannot get in on Android**: the button is iPhone-only, as ruled. Adding it later needs Apple's web flow, a Services ID and a secret that expires every six months. It should reach the same accounts, because Apple's user id is per developer team; not measured.
(10) ⚠️ **Somebody with a Google login who taps Apple and hides their email gets a second, empty account.** The welcome screen's *Cerrar sesión* is now the way back. Sharing the same address should land in the first account (Supabase joins logins that share a verified address); read in its documentation, never measured, and `5R-k-b` measures it.
(11) ⚠️ **A message to a hidden address arrives only if its sender is registered with Apple's relay.** The aviso's section 9 promises an email to every account. No hidden-address account exists today; `5R-k-b`'s row carries it.

That needs a hand on a phone; `5R-d-iv`'s row carries it on.
**What shipped:** `docs/runbooks/engagement.sql`, one read-only statement. `supabase db query --linked -o table -f docs/runbooks/engagement.sql` prints one row per store per day for the last 14 days, newest first: how many sales, purchases and write-offs were recorded, zeros included. No migration, nothing in the app, nothing a shopkeeper sees. `supabase/tests/runbook_engagement.sql` drives that same file against invented shops in `db.yml`'s *supabase db reset* job, and `db.yml` now fires when the runbook changes.
**What the estimate found (`S`, built whole):**
(1) **The aviso already fixes the columns.** Finalidad 9: *"Son conteos por tienda, no por persona."* So the read has no column about people, and the suite's first check pins the list.
(2) **A day is the store's day** (`location.timezone`), and *today* is a different date in different stores. A sale at 23:30 in Mexico City is already tomorrow in UTC.
(3) **A silent day has to be a row.** A plain count returns nothing for a day with nothing in it, and silence is what this read exists to show. So it builds each store's calendar, starting the day the store was created: a shop that signed up and never recorded is listed, as zeros.
(4) **A cancellation is a second row in the same table** (`reversal_of`, `0021`), on its own day. Counted, one mistake reads as two sales. The cancelled document counts where it was recorded; the cancellation does not.
(5) ⚠️ **A recent zero can still fill in.** A phone without signal sends its day when it reconnects, and the sale counts on the day it was made. Offline for more than 72 hours, it lands 72 hours before the reconnection (`0016`'s clamp). The server cannot tell an idle shop from an offline one; the runbook's header says so.
(6) ⚠️⚠️ **Found only by running the real command: the CLI prints a Postgres `date` as `2026-10-13 00:00:00 +0000 UTC`**, on the one read whose day is not UTC. psql prints a date as a date, so the suite was green over it. The day is returned as text, and the suite now pins that.
(7) ⚠️ **The count cannot tell a pilot shop from a store shop**, and the aviso files it under the pilot. Parked in ⛔ above, against `5R-e`.
**Evidence, local:** the suite's own tally, *all 21 checks passed*, both ways it can load the runbook: handed in by the caller, and read from the working directory as CI does. With the file missing, psql exits 3 before anything is asserted. **Falsified: 12 broken copies of the runbook, 12 red**: the day cut in UTC; one zone for every store; the cancellation counted; zero days dropped; the calendar ignoring the creation day; a window one day too long; a column about people; *today* taken from the server's clock; a shop's stores collapsed into one; purchases read from the sales table; the day returned as a date; the file emptied. ⚠️ Two of the suite's four stores sit at UTC−11 and UTC+14, where no shop is, so the *today* break is red at whatever hour CI runs; with Mexican zones alone it would be red only after 18:00.
**Evidence, the hosted project** (a read, through the Management API): the command returned 14 rows for the one shop there, with 3 sales, 2 purchases and 0 write-offs in the window. A second count written without the calendar gave the same three numbers.
⚠️ **Not proven: two real shops side by side.** The hosted project has one shop and one store. More than one shop, more than one store in a shop, and a second timezone are proven on fixtures only.
**Decided on his behalf, each an edit to one file, none a migration:** 14 days (one number at the top of the file). Counted per STORE, named *shop · store* when the two names differ. A cancelled document counts and its cancellation does not. A store with nothing recorded is listed from its first day. Column names in English, the day as text. ⚠️ **The session ran the read on the hosted project**, where this row said the owner would: reading it once is how the command was proven. **Not built: a summary per shop** (*recorded on 11 of 14 days*). ✅ **2026-10-10 — `5R-b` IS SPLIT, `5R-b-i` IS DONE, `5R-b-ii` WAITS ON THE OWNER, AND `5R-d` IS THE NEXT TASK (the listing and the aviso).**
**`5R-b-i`, everything the cloud build and over-the-air updates need that no Expo account does — no migration, nothing a shopkeeper sees.** `expo-updates` 57.0.25;
`runtimeVersion: { policy: 'fingerprint' }` in `app.json` and `app/fingerprint.config.js`; `app/eas.json` with `preview` (internal, an Android APK) and `production`
(auto-incremented build numbers, kept on EAS); and `docs/runbooks/ship-an-update.sh <channel> "<message>" [--check]`, the one command that ships a fix.
**What the estimate found (`M` as written, split for two non-size reasons):** (1) **the native risk came first and was small.** EAS runs a fresh `prebuild` and a **default**
`pod install`, which is not how `app/ios` on this Mac was made (the simulator needs RN from source). A clean prebuild with default pods **compiled for an iPhone**
(`xcodebuild` Release, `generic/platform=iOS`, unsigned, `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **` in 237 s) **and for Android** (`assembleRelease`, `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`; the first attempt's
Gradle daemon died while both builds ran at once, and a rerun finished it). (2) ⚠️⚠️ **The real work was the fingerprint, and without it every update would have reached
nobody.** eas-cli computes the runtime on the Mac that runs it; the cloud builder computes it again on a fresh install and **throws** if they differ (`@expo/build-tools`
24.12.1: *"Runtime version calculated on local machine not equal to runtime version calculated during build"*). And `eas update` **never compares its runtime with any build**
(read in eas-cli 24.12.1's source): one computed wrong publishes to a runtime no phone has and reports success. Three things moved the hash with no edit by anyone:
**`version`/`buildNumber`** (EAS's auto-increment would have split identical builds), **a local Android build rewriting `@react-native-masked-view`'s `AndroidManifest.xml` inside
`node_modules`**, and **Kotlin's `.kotlin/` session files**. The config skips the first and ignores the other two. **Measured after the fix:** a fresh `npm ci` checkout and this Mac's
`node_modules` (rewritten by weeks of local builds) give the **same hash on iOS and Android**. With the manifest ignore removed they split again (the falsifier).
(3) **Two halves, one of them invisible here:** the login, the cloud build, another person's phone and a timed update are `5R-b-ii`, gated on the decisions block.
⚠️ **Corrected in-session:** I first read the rewritten manifest as a hand edit made on 2026-09-21. It is not one: the clean checkout's copy was rewritten at 00:34 by this session's own Gradle run.
**Decisions taken for him, all edits, none a migration:** the fingerprint policy and its skips; the channel names `preview`/`production`, each bundled from the EAS environment
of the same name (`app/test/release.test.ts` holds that); build numbers kept on EAS; `preview` Android as an APK. Updates use the `expo-updates` default: **a phone runs a fix
on its second cold start after publishing, with no message, and never waits for the network to open** (`EXUpdatesLaunchWaitMs` 0, read off the built `Expo.plist`).
The runbook refuses a dirty tree, a commit not on `origin/main`, a missing `EXPO_PUBLIC_` value on EAS, and an update no finished build can run. No `.easignore` (18 MB tracked).
No Node pin on EAS, because its default could not be read without an account.
**Evidence:** Vitest **1771/1771 in 56 files** (`release.test.ts` 5 of 5, red when `preview`'s environment is renamed), typecheck clean, `conventions-gate.sh` 18 of 18. The runbook's
seven local refusals were run, ending at *"not logged in to Expo"* on a clean `origin/main` checkout. ⚠️ **Not tested: its EAS-side refusals**, which need the login.
**Nothing to look at on a phone and nothing to rebuild**: no screen changed, and the 9 October build on his iPhone has no updates URL, so it receives nothing until `5R-b-ii` rebuilds it.

✅ **2026-10-09 — `5R-c` IS DONE AND `5R-h` IS THE NEXT TASK (*¿Olvidaste tu contraseña?* by WhatsApp, ruled).**
**`5R-c`, account deletion: `0053_account_deletion.sql`** and a *Tu cuenta* section at the bottom of Ajustes. The migration drops the **eleven** foreign keys to `auth.users`
(the row said ten plus `workspace_member`; the database counts eleven) and keeps every column. It adds `workspace_member.left_at`, stamped by a trigger whenever `is_active` goes false.
It adds a trigger so a NEW membership must still name a living login, and `delete_my_account(p_shop_name)`.
**What the estimate found (L, one sitting, built whole):** (1) **the risk the row named first was real but small.** A shop with history can be deleted in one transaction:
the self-referencing `restrict` keys are fine inside single statements, and the deferred receipt check passes at commit. Measured on the seed (660 sales, 2,649 movements).
What refuses is three **triggers** (the ledger's immutability, the generic provider, prebuilt catalog rows). They now step aside only for the one workspace
`delete_my_account` is deleting, behind a transaction-local setting no client can set. (2) **Dropping `workspace_member.user_id`'s key opened a hole the row did not name:** an access
token outlives its deleted account by up to an hour, and with no key it could have opened a new shop. The login-exists trigger closes it (check 3.5). (3) The only place an actor's
name reaches a person today is the month export's *Quién* column, so that is where *"(ex-miembro)"* lands.
**Decisions taken for him:** a sole owner's typed name is checked **on the server too** (`TD007`, minted; `TD008` is next). The name comparison folds case and spacing but **not
accents**, like `normalize_name`. A former member with no stored name gets the login's `full_name` frozen onto the row, else the export says *Ex-miembro*. **Invitations he SENT stay;
those addressed to him go.** Memberships already inactive get `left_at = updated_at` (the hosted project has **none**, measured). The Spanish copy is mine. Its most consequential sentence
is the sole owner's: *"Eres la única persona dueña de {tienda}. Si eliminas tu cuenta, se borra la tienda completa…"*. The export offer opens Números rather than making a file in place.
⚠️ **Found, not fixed:** `workspace_member_delete` still lets an owner **hard-delete** a membership through the API, which would erase that person's pilot readings. No screen does it,
and removing a member is not built. When it is, his ruling says it is the tombstone (`is_active` false), and that is the moment to narrow the policy.
⚠️ **An unsent sale on the phone of somebody deleting their account** is not handled: deletion needs the network, and the network drains the outbox first.
**Evidence:** `supabase/tests/0053_account_deletion.sql` **45 of 45**, after seven falsifications. One falsification found **the suite's own defect**: checks recorded inside a rolled-back
transaction vanished, and the report still said "all passed". It now refuses a short count. Five older suites keep inventories that `0053` correctly changed, and they are updated:
`0026` 1.5, `0027` 7.3, `0035` 6.1, `0037` 5.3 and `0038` 7.2. After a clean `supabase db reset`, all **32** behavioural suites (the `db.yml` log), the **7** pgTAP suites and the **11** seed checks pass (the runners' tallies).
`docs/checks/5R-c-account-deletion-contract.sh` **7 of 7** over HTTP, and its falsifier **3 of 3**. Vitest **1758/1758 in 54 files**, typecheck clean, `conventions-gate.sh` 18 of 18.
⚠️ **Not looked at:** the section itself on a phone (R9). It reaches his phone with the next rebuild.

✅ **2026-10-09 — PLANNING, NO CODE: `5R-c` IS RULED AND IS THE NEXT TASK; THE SIGN-IN GAPS ARE ROWS (`5R-h`, `5R-i`, `5R-j`).**
⚠️⚠️ **HIS RULINGS ON ACCOUNT DELETION, 2026-10-09, after an assessment in-session.** He chose the alternative that **keeps the name**:
(1) **A member who deletes their account loses their LOGIN** (email, password, Google link, sessions, invite emails), but **the shop keeps a former-member record**: the display name,
the role, and when they joined and left. Every sale, void and waste they recorded still reads *"María (ex-miembro)"*. The reasoning: the shop is the data controller of its own staff
records under LFPDPPP, and Wera holds them for it; the books are the merchant's. **The same tombstone covers an owner removing a member.** (2) **A sole owner's deletion deletes
the shop**, after a warning that names who loses access, an offer of the monthly export, and the shop's name typed to confirm. If there is a second owner, they simply leave.
(3) **Immediate**, no grace period. Backups keep it for their retention window, and the aviso says so. (4) **When a shop is deleted, nothing is kept for analytics**, not even
anonymous aggregates. (5) **In-app now**; Google's web request page ships with `5R-d`.
**A phone number, if `5R-i` ever verifies one, is login data and goes with the login. The tombstone keeps the name, never the phone** (the proportionality principle).
⚠️⚠️ **CHEAP NOW, EXPENSIVE LATER — the shape `5R-c` must build:** the ten references to `auth.users` (`sale`, `purchase`, `waste`, `stock_batch`, `stock_movement`,
`failed_write` ×2, `workspace_invite` ×3) are **dropped as constraints, keeping the uuid**, and `workspace_member.user_id` stops cascading. **No ledger row is ever rewritten.**
After the first real deletion those constraints can never return, because the ids they would point at are gone.
**Measured in-session (read-only, hosted):** 3 users, all Google, all with an email, none with a phone. Email + password sign-up is ON with **confirmation OFF** (`mailer_autoconfirm`),
which is his wish for now. No custom SMTP (the built-in sender allows about 2 emails an hour). **The app has no password reset.** **Nobody has ever signed up by email on the hosted project.**
**His ruling on the reset gap, 2026-10-09:** *¿Olvidaste tu contraseña?* opens **WhatsApp to the owner**, and **he resets the password by hand** (`5R-h`). His number is kept OUT of this
public repository: it lives in the gitignored `app/.env.local` and the session's private memory.
**Parked by him, as rows so they are not lost:** phone verification (`5R-i`); email verification with a real mail provider and a self-service reset (`5R-j`).
**The order, set in-session:** `5R-c` (L), then `5R-h` (S), then `5R-b` (M), then `5R-d` (M), then `5R-e`. `5R-c` comes first on his instruction (*"let's have that addressed now"*).
`5R-h` follows because it is small and closes a hole any email user can fall into. `5R-b` and `5R-d` are the rest of the store path. `8g`/`8h` stay parked on his word.

tienda* is the import button: no giro chosen means *Omitir*. After a later import the route goes back, and Productos is
the confirmation. **Evidence:** Vitest **1741/1741 in 53 files** (the runner's tally); `conventions-gate.sh` 18 of 18;
`9d-starter-catalog-contract.sh` **10 of 10** over HTTP, run after `6c` the way CI orders them; its falsifier **4 of 4**,
each red for its reason; typecheck clean after regenerating the typed routes. **Looked at** on the iPhone 17 simulator
(Release, local shop, bundle checked to hold the local URL and no production ref): onboarding offers *Pollería · 24
productos*; Lupe's Productos shows the entry; her picker offers 23, because one product is already hers by name. ⚠️ **NOT
looked at: the untick list itself.** Reaching it takes a tap, and macOS refuses synthetic input to this terminal, so it
is **the owner's phone, R9**, together with the list's length and whether *Volver* should ask before discarding a choice.
No migration. **It reaches his phone with the next rebuild**: 4 October, or the paid-team build.
**`9e`, the Carnicería session with the owner, the same day: `0046_catalogo_carniceria.sql`**, generated, **54 new products**
in 3 new families (*Res* 29 and *Cerdo* 24, by the kilo; *Tuétano* 1, by the piece) plus *Huevo blanco* and *Huevo rojo*
**newly tagged** Carnicería. That makes 78 in the template, and 3 new category tags (*Cortes*, *Embutidos*, *Manteca*). ⚠️⚠️ **HIS RULINGS, 2026-10-01:**
(1) **A carnicería sells eggs, not chicken.** (2) **Grades are products**: *Molida de res* and *Molida de cerdo* each in
**90/10, 85/15, 80/20, 75/25, 70/30, lean first**, and *Bistec* and *Milanesa* of both animals *de primera* and *de segunda*.
They replace the plain rows. (3) **IVA 0 on everything**, the marinated and *embutidos* included. (4) **Tuétano is sold by the
piece, so it is a family of its own**, because C8.5 keeps one measure per family. *Pata de res* and *Manitas de cerdo* are by the kilo.
(5) Chorizo and Longaniza go under *Embutidos*, and *Manteca de cerdo* has a category of its own. ⚠️ **Names carry the animal**
(*Hígado de res*, *Pierna de cerdo*) because `product_variant_name_unique` is shop-wide, and Pollería already holds *Hígado*,
*Pierna*, *Retazo*. **Guessed and kept unchanged:** *Sirloin* and *Rib eye* as names, and every unit except Tuétano's.
**Left out:** carnero, borrego and other meats (*"not by the moment"*). Lifespans are blank. **Measured locally:** a fresh
shop imports 56 of 56, then 0 a second time (56 skipped); Pollería afterwards imports 22 and skips only the 2 eggs, so no
name collides across giros; `cat-embutidos` alone finds its 2 already in. All 31 behavioural suites pass with the
content present; `build.test.mjs` is 20 of 20 after the new migration.

☑️ **`8f` IS DONE, 2026-09-30** — and `8g` was then the next task, the owner's screen-by-screen review. His four rulings (asked, not decided): **light only** (`app.json`); **majority wins** for
the drifted pieces; **Precios' value under its word**; and headers, the tab colour and the first two screens
**are his, screen by screen** — so `8f` did not touch them. Built: `src/ui/BotonLleno` (**13** hand-drawn
filled buttons in 11 files, not the 9 on record — the three *Cancelar* gained the border the other ten had)
and `src/ui/Velo` (**13** veils in 6 files, now always fading in — six used to appear at once); the chosen
pill's 3 on bienvenida is 2 like the other five (Familia had none — the map was wrong there); busy fades 0.6
everywhere; `smallSize` names the 33 `bodySize * 0.85`; the cart timings live in `theme/pulse.ts`.
⚠️ **The empty band above *Hoy* was NOT removed, though the question offered it** — it is the dead-letter
banner's reserved room (`bannerRoom`), a ruling of its own. **Vitest 1719/1719 in 52 files;
`conventions-gate.sh` 18 groups, R14 counting 13 components; its falsifier 35/35.** **Looked at** on the iPhone 17
simulator (Release, local seed shop, production untouched): Precios at both sizes — no word breaks — and
Ajustes' filled buttons. The veil's fade and the *Cancelar* border are the owner's phone's to judge. No migration.

## Step 5R — the release path, and it is now a STEP (§2.11)

⚠️⚠️ **PROMOTED FROM PROSE TO TASKS ON 2026-09-21, ON THE OWNER'S INSTRUCTION, AND THE
TARGET CHANGED WITH IT.** §2.11 carries the release path as a paragraph and says *"it
is not a step because it blocks nothing"*. **That was true when the pilot's output was
a measurement. It is false now that the pilot's output is also a thing shown to
investors and partners** — the owner ruled on 2026-09-21 that the app ships to **both
public stores**, not to TestFlight and Play internal testing alone.

⚠️ **The recommendation on file was internal distribution only**, on the grounds that
App Store review sits inside §5's three-day observation window. **The owner overrode
it with a reason the recommendation had not weighed: a public listing is evidence to a
partner, and this pilot exists to raise money as well as to measure completeness.**
Recorded here rather than argued again.

⚠️ **§2.11's acceptance test survives the change and is not optional:** an over-the-air
JS-only fix shipped to a real device in under an hour, proven **before** pilot day.
Public review governs binaries; it does not govern OTA updates, so this is what keeps
a 48-hour review out of the observation window.

⚠️⚠️ **THE IDS ARE `5R-x` AND `5P-x` RATHER THAN `R1`/`P1`, AND THAT IS A CORRECTION MADE
THE SAME DAY.** They were first written `R1`–`R5` and `P1`–`P3`, and **every one of the
eight collided with a row that already existed** — `R1`–`R13` are `docs/CONVENTIONS.md`'s
RULE names (`R12` and `R13` are cited across the plan and read by
`conventions-gate.sh`), and `P1`–`P3` are falsification fixtures in the `5a-iv` tables.
⚠️ **A grep for `R3` returned a store gate and an empty-env-var fixture**, which is the
one-identifier-two-meanings defect this file has recorded several times. The `5` prefix
also says something true: §2.11 puts the release path *"running in parallel from 5a"*.
